- Core commands: Docker mode start and stop
	- ID: 2026111112163174
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Needs hand testing: start and stop a throwaway Paper server in Docker mode, with the EULA accepted by hand. It loads, `stop --warn 10` counts down and saves, and the jars can't be written.
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-121631
	- Opened by: t00mietum
	- Parent ID: 2026111111065711
	- Prereq IDs: 2026111112162268
	- Requirements:
		- `start` and `stop` in Docker mode, per `design.md`, through the runner, with `--debug`, `--foreground`, and `--warn N`.
		- Refuse to start when a Paper server is already running, under any account.
		- `stop` sends the `stop` console command first, and hands the trees back even after a crash.
		- Test with the fake runner and `--dry-run` only.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Progress log:
		- Verified: the same `docker run` flags on a small image give a writable `/tmp`, read-only jars and image, and umask 0007. The console's `stop` reached a real container's terminal.
		- 20261111: Verified on a throwaway Paper 26.3-249 server with Geyser and Floodgate, `eula.txt` written beforehand: `start` loaded it in 18 s. In the container, `paper.jar` and the plugin jars refused writes, a plugin jar couldn't be moved, the tree took writes, and the server ran with umask 0007. `stop --warn 10` said 10, 5, 4, 3, 2, and 1 seconds in the chat, Paper saved the worlds and exited 0, and the trees went back. A second `start` removed the stopped container first.
		- Stand-in: sudo passed the command straight through, as the operator, who is in the `docker` group, so Docker itself ran as root.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: Docker mode start and stop passed on b23 at r1112 b47154b.
	- Decisions:
		- `stop` writes to the container's input through the Docker API, as `cmd` will, since `docker attach` refuses input that isn't a terminal. A line ends with a carriage return, which a cooked terminal takes, and a raw one such as Paper's should too.
		- The countdown says the time left at the start, then at 60, 30, 10, and 5 to 1 seconds.
		- "Already running" comes from `ps`: any `java -jar paper*.jar`, under any account. That covers the old scripts and this tool's own container.
		- The server runs under `sh -c 'umask 0007 && exec "$@"'` in the image, since Docker has no umask option. The pid limit is 2048, for Paper's thread pools.
		- `--debug` runs attached and hands the trees back when the server exits. `--foreground` runs detached, so `console` still works, then waits with `docker wait`.
		- A start that times out leaves the server running, with an error that says so.
		- The safe settings, plugin, and firewall steps of `start` come with their own items. ZFS snapshots had none, so 2026111112264283 is filed.
		- Swept: every container command uses `launch.Container`; `start` and `stop` both hand back through `tree.ToOperator`.
	- Branch: r1111
	- Test case: 1a3egrr, 1a3egvw, 1a3egz0, 1a3eh24, 1a3eh58, 1a3eh8c, 1a3ehbg, 1a3ehhr, 1a3ehmw
	- Closed: 20261111-181653

- Core commands: status and watch
	- ID: 2026111112163393
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
		- The native mode part moved to 2026111112163284's hand test, since it needs that server.
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Needs hand testing: run `status` and `watch` beside the throwaway Paper server from the Docker and native hand tests. Version, players, memory, ports, and the log tail show, and Ctrl-C ends `watch`.
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-121633
	- Opened by: t00mietum
	- Parent ID: 2026111111065711
	- Prereq IDs: 2026111112163174
	- Requirements:
		- `status` and `watch`, per `design.md`. Parts that need a later item, such as the firewall counters, show as not set up yet.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Progress log:
		- Verified: against a throwaway container named for the tool, `status` showed it running, its account, its time up, and its memory from the cgroup, then exited once stopped.
		- 20261111: Verified beside a throwaway Paper 26.3-249 server in Docker mode: `status` showed docker mode, the account, the time up, Paper 26.3 with its jar, 0 of 20 players from the ping, the memory against its cap, the game port over TCP and the Bedrock port over TCP and UDP, the whitelist, ops, approved jars, trees, and the log tail. `watch` redrew it 7 times in 12 s, and Ctrl-C ended it with exit 0 and the server still up.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: Docker mode status and watch passed on b23 at r1112 b47154b. Native mode goes with 2026111112163284.
	- Decisions:
		- Players and the version come from a server list ping on `bindIP`, or 127.0.0.1 when it's all addresses. The Paper build comes from the jar `paper.jar` links to, so it shows when stopped too.
		- Memory is the cgroup's `memory.current`: the container's, found through its pid, or the unit's `MemoryCurrent`. Both work without sudo.
		- Ports come from `ss`, which can't name another account's process without root, so it shows the address that listens and the TCP connection count.
		- The log is `logs/latest.log` in both modes, since `docker logs` of a terminal holds escape codes. Control characters are dropped from it and from op names.
		- The server owns the tree, so files that aren't plain files are not read, and a jar that's a pipe or a device counts as changed. design.md updated.
		- The plugin check is `plugins.CheckJars`, for `start`'s check to reuse. `watch` hashes a jar again only when its size or time changes.
		- The approved list is read from beside the config, so a second `--config` gets its own. design.md updated.
		- A part that can't be read says why, and the rest still shows. Exit 0, since `status` ran. A failed command's stderr goes into its line, not over the screen, through `host.Exec.QuietErr`.
		- No color yet. design.md allows it on a terminal only, and nothing needs it.
		- 20261111: `TestMissingConfig` (1a3egnm) no longer expects `status` to say it isn't built. It checks the config path `status` reads beside instead.
		- Swept: every file read from the tree goes through `openRegular` or `Sums.Sum`; both modes judge "running" through the same calls as `start`, `launch.ContainerState` and `launch.UnitUp`.
	- Branch: r1111
	- Test case: 1a3ejdw, 1a3ejh0, 1a3ejm4, 1a3ejq8, 1a3ejtc, 1a3ejxg, 1a3ek0m, 1a3ek3r, 1a3ek6w, 1a3eka0, 1a3ekd4. 1a3ek3r is commented out since 2026111914334569, which removed CheckJars.
	- Closed: 20261111-181653

- console and cmd
	- ID: 2026111111065750
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
		- The native mode part moved to 2026111112163284's hand test, since it needs that server.
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Needs hand testing: on a throwaway Paper server, in each mode, `console` shows the console, typed commands work, and Ctrl-C leaves it running. `cmd list` prints the player count.
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-110657
	- Opened by: t00mietum
	- Prereq IDs: 2026111112163174
	- Requirements:
		- `console` attaches to the server console, with detach keys that can't stop the server by accident.
		- `cmd` sends one console command and prints what the server says back.
		- Make both work in Docker and native modes, without RCON.
	- Prereq moved from the Core commands parent to its Docker mode child, which is all this needs to start.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Progress log:
		- Verified: against a stand-in container named like the server's, `console` detaches on Ctrl-C with the container still running, and `cmd` prints the line the stand-in logged. Without the detach keys, Ctrl-C ends the container. In tmux, Ctrl-C with the binding detaches and the session lives; without it the session ends.
		- 20261111: Verified on a throwaway Paper 26.3-249 server, in Docker mode and then rootless Podman: `console` showed Paper's console, a typed `list` got its reply, and Ctrl-C detached with exit 0 and the server still up. `cmd list` printed "There are 0 of a max of 20 players online".
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: console and cmd passed on b23 at r1112 b47154b, in Docker and Podman modes. Native mode goes with 2026111112163284.
	- Decisions:
		- Ctrl-C is the detach key in both modes, so it can't reach the server. Docker's `--detach-keys=ctrl-c`, and in tmux `bind-key -n C-c detach-client` at attach. design.md updated.
		- `docker attach` exits 1 after its detach keys, so that exit with the server still up counts as a detach.
		- `cmd` reads `logs/latest.log` in both modes, from where it ended before the send, rather than `docker logs`, which carries the terminal's echo and escape codes. It waits up to 3 s for a first line, then until 0.5 s go by with nothing, 10 s at most. A new day's log is read from its start.
		- `cmd stop` and `cmd restart` are refused, pointing at `stop`, which hands the trees back too.
		- A server that stops while `console` is attached gets its trees handed back, as `start --debug` in native mode did. Both now share one step. It no longer hands back when the unit's state can't be read, since the server may still be up.
		- `cmd` takes no `--dry-run`, since design.md lists none.
		- `OpenRegular` and `Clean` moved from `status` to `server`, for `cmd` to share.
		- Swept: both modes' console and cmd go through `Kit.session` and `Kit.command`; `status`, native start, and `cmd` all name the log through `server.LogFile`.
		- 20261111: Kept after the Check: Ctrl-C detaches in both modes, `cmd stop` and `cmd restart` are refused, and `cmd` keeps its wait times.
	- Branch: r1111
	- Test case: 1a3ener, 1a3enhw, 1a3enn0
	- Closed: 20261111-181653

- Run modes: console input through attach
	- ID: 2026111116104058
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Needs hand testing: on a throwaway Paper server under Podman, then Docker, with the operator out of the `docker` group: `cmd list` prints the player count, `stop --warn 10` counts down in the chat and stops it, and `console` detaches on Ctrl-C with the server still up.
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-161040
	- Opened by: t00mietum
	- Parent ID: 2026111115090766
	- Prereq IDs: 2026111116104049
	- Requirements:
		- `stop` and `cmd` send console lines with `sudo <runtime> attach` on a pseudo-terminal, in place of the Docker API write. See the parent's notes.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Progress log:
		- Verified: against throwaway busybox containers, rootless Podman 5.4.2 and Docker each got 2 lines in a row, detached, and stayed running.
		- Verified: Ctrl-C written right after the line went to the stand-in in the same read, which is how attach passes it on to the server. The console now waits until the line has been read.
		- 20261111: Verified on a throwaway Paper 26.3-249 server, under rootless Podman and under Docker: `cmd list` printed the player count, `stop --warn 10` counted down in the chat and stopped it, and `console` took a typed `list` and detached on Ctrl-C with the server still up.
		- Stand-in: sudo passed `attach` straight through, as the operator. The parent's 20261111 hand check covers `sudo <runtime> attach` rootful under both runtimes.
	- Acceptance signoff: Self-closed: console lines through attach passed against Paper's own console on b23 at r1112 b47154b; rootful attach through sudo was checked by hand under the parent.
	- Decisions:
		- `launch.AttachConsole` replaces `DockerConsole`. It opens its own pty with `O_NOCTTY`, sets it raw before attach starts, and runs `sudo -n`, so sudo reuses the operator's terminal. As root it runs the runtime directly.
		- Ctrl-C goes once the pty's input queue is empty, after the line was seen in it or 100 ms went by, since the pty hands bytes on a moment after they're written.
		- Docker's exit 1 counts as a detach only with "read escape sequence" on stderr. Podman exits 0. An attach that ends before reading the line is an error.
		- One line may take 15 s at most, attach and detach included. Attach's copy of the console is read and dropped, so it can't block on a full pty.
		- `Boxed` sets its console once it knows the runtime, so `stop` and `cmd` follow a container found under the other one.
		- `TestDockerConsole` (1a3ehbg) is now `TestAttachConsole` under the same ID, since the API write is gone. It still checks that the line arrives with a carriage return, that a control character is refused, and the dry run.
		- Swept: `stop`, `cmd`, and the countdown all send through `Kit.Console`; nothing reads `DOCKER_HOST` or the Docker socket now.
	- Branch: r1112
	- Test case: 1a3ehbg
	- Closed: 20261111-181653

- setup: download Paper
	- ID: 2026111116415186
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-164151
	- Opened by:
	- Parent ID: 2026111111065776
	- Related IDs: 2026111111065803
	- Requirements:
		- On a new tree, get the newest stable Paper build from the PaperMC download API, check its SHA-256, and point `paper.jar` at it, the same way `update --paper` does.
		- Share the code with `update --paper`.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Progress log:
		- Verified: against the live API, the newest release was 26.3, build 249, and the 55 MB jar it downloaded matched the API's SHA-256, with `paper.jar` linked to it.
		- Verified: 1a3f9vw and 1a3f9z0 fail with the stable filter or the hash check taken out, and pass with them in.
	- Decisions:
		- The API is PaperMC's Fill v3 at `fill.papermc.io`. The old v2 API is deprecated.
		- A new tree gets the newest release that has a stable build. Release candidates and versions with only beta builds are skipped.
		- The lookup comes before the plan, so a failed one stops setup before anything changes. The plan names the version and build.
		- The jar's size and SHA-256 must match the API's. It's written to a temp name in the tree and renamed, and `paper.jar` is replaced by a new link in one rename, all relative to the folder's handle without following links, since the server account may own the tree.
		- The build goes on the approved list, since `start` pins the jar `paper.jar` links to. A tree with a `paper.jar` keeps it, and setup approves it with the other jars. design.md updated.
		- The code is `source/internal/paper`, for `update --paper` to reuse. Keeping the last 2 builds is left to update.
		- Swept: nothing else downloads yet, and `plugins.Jars` already follows the `paper.jar` link.
	- Branch: r1112
	- Test case: 1a3f9rr, 1a3f9vw, 1a3f9z0, 1a3f8wm, 1a3f92w, 1a3f9c8
	- Closed: 20261111-165545

- setup: Geyser and Floodgate
	- ID: 2026111116415196
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-164151
	- Opened by:
	- Parent ID: 2026111111065776
	- Prereq IDs: 2026111116415186
	- Related IDs: 2026111111065803, 2026111117052190, 2026111117052206
	- Requirements:
		- With `bedrock` on, download Geyser and Floodgate, check their hashes, approve them, and set them up per design.md's Bedrock players.
	- Shares the download and hash check with 2026111116415186, and `update --plugins` reuses it.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Progress log:
		- 2026-11-11: With `bedrock` on, setup gets the newest Geyser and Floodgate builds from GeyserMC's download API before the plan, downloads them into `plugins/`, checks each SHA-256, and approves them with their source.
		- Verified: the live API's latest Geyser 2.12.1 build 1300 and Floodgate 2.2.5 build 141 Spigot jars, fetched from the version and build address, match the API's SHA-256.
		- Verified: 1a3faem, 1a3fahr, 1a3famw, 1a3f8wm, 1a3f92w, and 1a3f9z0 each fail with their check taken out, and pass with it in.
		- 20261111: 1a3f8wm `TestNewInstall` expects 3 approved jars, was 1. 1a3f92w `TestAdopt` expects 4, was 2, and finds `paper.jar` anywhere in the list. Both now get Geyser and Floodgate too.
		- 2026-11-12: Geyser's config is now written before its first start, through 2026111117052206. Only Floodgate's is left to its first start.
	- Decisions:
		- The download, size cap, and hash check moved from `source/internal/paper` to `source/internal/download`, which `paper` and the new `source/internal/geyser` share. Paper's behavior is the same.
		- The API is `download.geysermc.org/v2`. Its `latest` answer is read, and the jar is fetched from the address naming that version and build, so the file can't be a newer build than the hash.
		- GeyserMC gives no size, so the 512 MiB cap is the only size check. The jar's name has to start with its project's name, since `repair` tells the plugins apart by name.
		- A tree that has a Geyser or Floodgate jar keeps it, and setup approves it with the other jars, as with `paper.jar`.
		- The approved entry gets the `latest` Spigot download address as its source, as design.md's example has it, for `update --plugins`. `plugins.Info` gained a `Source`.
		- A missing `plugins/` is made mode 0770, owned like `serverDir`.
		- Geyser's and Floodgate's configs are left to their first start, per 2026111112554261. Whether Geyser's first config passes the next start is 2026111117052206.
		- `repair`'s Geyser and Floodgate check now uses `geyser.Installed`, so setup and repair agree on what counts as installed.
		- Swept: `paper.Install` and `geyser.Install` both go through `download.Client.Into`; `repair` and setup share `geyser.Installed`; the only other `plugins.Approve` callers pass no source.
	- Branch: r1112
	- Test case: 1a3faem, 1a3fahr, 1a3famw, 1a3f8wm, 1a3f92w, 1a3f9z0
	- Closed: 20261111-170548

- Firewall: rules file, its unit, and the start check
	- ID: 2026111117134046
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-171340
	- Opened by:
	- Parent ID: 2026111111065789
	- Requirements:
		- Render `/etc/tt-minecraftd/firewall.nft` from the config, and `tt-minecraftd-firewall.service` that loads it, per design.md's Firewall.
		- Tests check the rendered rules for each mix of `allowFrom`, `egress`, and `bedrock`.
		- With `firewall` on, `start` checks through sudo that the table is loaded, and refuses without it.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Progress log:
		- 2026-11-11: `source/internal/firewall` renders `firewall.nft` and the unit. Both modes' `start` refuse without the table, after the plugin check.
		- Verified: each rendered mix passes `nft -c`. A file loaded for real in a network namespace dropped and counted the account's connection to loopback, and let its DNS to 127.0.0.53 through. 1a3fbxg fails on a v4 match against a v6 set, and 1a3fc6w fails with the start check taken out.
	- Decisions:
		- The account is matched by name, so the file reads plainly. nft looks it up at load, after `setup` has made it.
		- The output hook only jumps to the account's rules on a uid match. A packet with no socket, such as IPv6 neighbor discovery, has no uid, so a `!=` match would have let it reach the LAN drop.
		- `lan` adds CGNAT's 100.64.0.0/10 to the private and link-local ranges, so a tailnet counts as LAN both ways. design.md updated.
		- The rate limit is per address, with a burst of `joinRateLimit`. IPv6 is per address too, not per /64.
		- DNS to the resolvers in `/etc/resolv.conf` is allowed first, so a loopback resolver still works. They're read at render time, so repair's check sees a change. design.md updated.
		- Closed ports are RCON's 25575/tcp and query on `gamePort`/udp. The management server has no fixed default port. design.md updated.
		- Only outbound drops are logged, at 6 a minute. Inbound ones are counted only, since scanners would fill the log. design.md updated.
		- The unit is `WantedBy` sysinit and nftables, `PartOf` and `ReloadPropagatedFrom` nftables, so it loads again after nftables starts, restarts, or reloads.
		- `start` says `repair` loads the table, which comes with 2026111117135246.
		- Swept: both modes' `Start` call `checkFirewall`; every start test's fake host answers the table list.
	- Branch: r1112
	- Test case: 1a3fbxg, 1a3fc0m, 1a3fc3r, 1a3fc6w
	- Closed: 20261111-172010

- Firewall: drop counters in status
	- ID: 2026111117135258
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Priority: Avg
	- Opened: 20261111-171352
	- Opened by:
	- Parent ID: 2026111111065789
	- Prereq IDs: 2026111117134046
	- Requirements:
		- `status` shows the firewall's drop counters, inbound and outbound, in place of "not set up yet".
	- The counters are the named ones in `firewall.nft`: `in_refused`, `in_flood`, `in_closed`, `out_local`, `out_lan`, and `out_web`. Reading them needs root, and `status` doesn't use sudo, so this needs a pick, such as `sudo -n` when it's cached, or "run with sudo to see them".
	- 2026-11-12: Picked: status shows the counters when run as root, and otherwise one line saying sudo shows them. No sudo -n, so the output doesn't change with sudo's cache.
	- Actual effort: Low
	- Progress log:
		- 2026-11-11: `firewall.Counters` reads the table's counters from `nft -j list counters inet`. As root, `status` shows them on 2 lines, dropped in and dropped out. Otherwise it says sudo shows them. design.md updated.
		- Verified: 1a3ffe0 and 1a3ffh4 pass, and both fail with the table match taken out. 1a3ffh4 parses nft's own answer, with the rules loaded in a network namespace and with none.
	- Decisions:
		- All inet counters are listed, not the table's, since nft answers that the same way when the table is gone. No counters of ours means the table isn't loaded, and `status` says `repair` loads it.
		- Packet counts only. `in_refused` is left out with `allowFrom` `any`, and `out_web` with `egress` `open`, since neither can count anything then.

		- Swept: `status` was the only place that said "not set up yet"; `firewall.Loaded` is still the only other reader of the table.
	- Branch: r1112
	- Test case: 1a3ffe0, 1a3ffh4, 1a3ejdw

- update: the command, and --paper
	- ID: 2026111117302950
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-173029
	- Opened by:
	- Parent ID: 2026111111065803
	- Requirements:
		- `update` per design.md: needs root through sudo, asks first unless `--yes`, updates every part when none is named, and leaves a running server alone.
		- `--paper` gets the newest stable build of the current Minecraft version, checks it, approves it, points `paper.jar` at it, and keeps the last 2 builds.
		- setup on an existing install runs `update` after `repair`.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Progress log:
		- Verified: against the live API, `update --dry-run` on a tree at Paper 26.3 build 244 planned the move to build 249.
		- Verified: 1a3fdjm, 1a3fdnr, 1a3fdrw, 1a3fdw0, and 1a3fdz4 each fail with their check taken out, such as the kept build, the approval, or the update after repair, and pass with it in.
	- Decisions:
		- The code is `source/internal/update`, and reuses `paper.API` and the approval setup does.
		- The version and build come from the name `paper.jar` links to. A plain `paper.jar` is refused with how to rename it, since swapping a link over it would delete the only copy. design.md updated.
		- No `paper.jar`, or a link to a build that's gone, gets one as setup does, which makes repair's missing paper.jar message true.
		- The 2 builds kept are the new one and the one `paper.jar` linked to. Only plain files in `serverDir` named `paper-VERSION-BUILD.jar` are removed. Their approved entries stay, since they're harmless.
		- Everything is looked up before the plan, so a failed lookup changes nothing.
		- With only `--paper` built, `update` with no part named is the same as `--paper`. The other flags come with their children.
		- On an existing install setup asks once, then runs `repair` and `update` without asking again. Update runs even when a repair check failed, since a missing `paper.jar` is one update fixes, and both errors are returned.
		- Swept: setup's new-install path and update share `paper.API` and `plugins.Approve`; `asRoot`'s message now fits both callers.
	- Branch: r1112
	- Test case: 1a3fdjm, 1a3fdnr, 1a3fdrw, 1a3fdw0, 1a3fdz4
	- Closed: 20261111-173642

- update --plugins
	- ID: 2026111117302973
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-173029
	- Opened by:
	- Parent ID: 2026111111065803
	- Requirements:
		- `--plugins` updates each approved jar with a known source, such as Geyser and Floodgate, shows the old and new versions, checks the source's hash, and approves the new jar.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Progress log:
		- Verified: against the live GeyserMC API, `update --dry-run` on an approved Geyser jar with an old hash planned the move to Geyser 2.12.1 build 1300.
		- Verified: 1a3fe28 fails with the file name check, the up-to-date check, or the missing jar check taken out, and passes with them in.
		- 2026-11-12: 1a3fe28 failed about 1 run in 10, since its rig approved the jars in map order, so Floodgate was sometimes looked up before Geyser and the failed lookup named Floodgate. The rig now approves them in a fixed order. Update itself follows the approved list's order.
		- Verified: 1a3fe28 failed 1 of 10 runs before the fix, and passed 25 runs in a row after it. The gate passes on both temp folders.
	- Decisions:
		- A jar is up to date when its approved hash is the source's newest, since GeyserMC's jar names have no version in them. The old version shown is what the jar's own `plugin.yml` says.
		- A newest build with another file name is skipped with a note, since both jars would load. So is an approved jar that's gone from the tree, so update never adds one back.
		- Only GeyserMC's `latest` sources are known. Modrinth and Hangar have no jars with a source yet, so they're 2026111117404480. Other sources are listed as skipped.

	- Branch: r1112
	- Test case: 1a3fe28, 1a3fdz4

- update --image
	- ID: 2026111117302985
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Priority: Avg
	- Opened: 20261111-173029
	- Opened by:
	- Parent ID: 2026111111065803
	- Requirements:
		- `--image` pulls the newest base image, checks that its `java -version` is new enough for this Paper, and pins its digest in the config.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Progress log:
		- Verified: 1a3fg3w fails with the Java check or the per-arch digest skip taken out, and passes with them in.
		- Verified: with the live PaperMC API and eclipse-temurin:25-jre, under both Podman and Docker without sudo, the pull, the Java check, and the pin all worked, and both runtimes pinned the same digest.
	- Decisions:
		- The Java needed comes from the PaperMC API's version info (`java.version.minimum`), for the version the server runs after this update. `paper.API` gained `Java`.
		- It follows the configured tag and never moves to another. An image with a digest and no tag is skipped. One with neither follows `latest`.
		- The pull waits for the go-ahead, since it's what finds the digest, so a dry run can't show the new one. A registry lookup was the other way, but it would bypass the runtime's own registry settings.
		- The pin is the tag's own (multi-arch) digest. Podman also lists the per-arch one, which it gives as `Digest`, and that's left out. Docker gives a short name, so names are compared without `docker.io/library/`.
		- `java -version` runs as nobody, with no network, read-only, and no caps.
		- Only the `image` line changes, through shcl's keep-lines save, since a plain save rewrites every key in lower case. shcl quotes the new value.
		- The old image isn't removed, since a running server may still use it. Native mode skips the part, and says so only when `--image` is named.
		- design.md's update section has the details.
		- Swept: `PaperSource` has the one real implementation; the test fakes in update and main got `Java`. setup's own `PaperSource` doesn't need it.
	- Branch: r1112
	- Test case: 1a3fg0r, 1a3fg3w, 1a3fg70, 1a3fga4, 1a3fdz4
	- Closed: 20261111-180421

- Native start misses the new latest.log when its inode is reused
	- ID: 2026111113354106
	- Type: Bug
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Priority: Avg
	- Opened: 20261111-133541
	- Opened by:
	- Related IDs: 2026111112163284, 2026111111065750
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]: TestNativeStart (1a3ehv4) fails with TMPDIR on an ext4 folder: start waits out startTimeout though the new latest.log has the Done line.
	- Expected behavior [Bug]: start sees the new log on any file system.
	- Reproduced [Bug]: 2026-11-11, on HEAD 2076b3a, with TMPDIR on ext4. Passes on ZFS.
	- Possible cause [Bug]: Native.log tells the old log from the new one with os.SameFile. ext4 hands a removed file's inode to the next new file, so the new log looks like the old one. Log4j's rollover deletes latest.log, so a real server could hit it too.
	- Actual cause [Bug]: As above. The old log's inode was free once it was removed, since nothing held it open. `cmd`'s log follower had the same flaw: a new day's log on a reused inode was read from the old offset.
	- Estimated effort: Low
	- Actual effort: Low
	- Progress log:
		- Verified: on ext4, 1a3ehv4 and 1a3eq0r fail on the old code and pass with the fix. The gate passes with the default TMPDIR and with TMPDIR on ext4.
	- Decisions:
		- Hold the old log open until the server has loaded, so its inode can't go to the new file. Then `os.SameFile` is sound on any file system. A size or time check would still guess.
		- `cmd`'s follower holds the file it read last, until the command ends.
		- Swept: `os.SameFile` is used only in `Native.log` and `logFollow.next`, and both are fixed.
	- Actual fix [Bug]: `Native` keeps `latest.log` open from before the launch until loaded; `logFollow` keeps its last file open.
	- Branch: r1111
	- Test case: 1a3ehv4, 1a3eq0r
	- Closed: 20261111-133909

- Config reader for shcl in Go
	- ID: 2026111111065684
	- Type: Task
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-110656
	- Opened by: t00mietum
	- Requirements:
		- Read `config.shcl` and `plugins.shcl` from Go.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Decisions:
		- Config is shcl. The tool reads it with the latest published release of shcl's Go module, beta or not.
		- shcl `v3.0.0-beta1`, the only v3 release. `go.mod` says Go 1.24, so Debian 13's Go builds it.
		- The reader lays the file over a `Config` the caller fills first, so the defaults item only has to fill it. Empty fields are left for that item to derive, such as `runGroup` from `runUser`.
		- A bad value, an unknown key, or a key set twice with different values gets a warning with its line, and the value before it stands. A list with one bad item is dropped whole. Only a file that can't be read is an error.
		- Values are checked here: paths for `chown -R` must be full and not `/`, names can't start with `-`, ports and `nice` stay in range, and `allowFrom` takes `lan`, `any`, addresses, or ranges.
		- Sizes take Java's `4G` and shcl's `4 GiB`, since shcl alone refuses `4G`.
		- `startAtBoot` is read here too, so every key in design.md's table is read. A test checks the two match.
		- In `plugins.shcl`, a jar is named by its path under `serverDir`. A bad entry is left out, so its jar isn't approved. shcl merges entries with the same path, so a jar listed with 2 hashes is left out too.
		- shcl's hints are dropped from the warnings. They repeat ours, and "did you mean a list" misleads for a key that takes one value.
		- Swept: every key in the table has a setter and a test line; both files go through the one `LoadFile`.
		- 20261111: The Check picks stand: jars named by their path, a key set twice with 2 values using neither, and a list with a bad item dropped whole.
	- Branch: r1111
	- Test case: 1a3ee18, 1a3ee4c, 1a3ee7g, 1a3eeam, 1a3eedr, 1a3eegw, 1a3eem0, 1a3eeq4, 1a3eet8, 1a3eexc, 1a3ef0g, 1a3ef3m
	- Closed: 20261111-120826

- Config defaults and overrides
	- ID: 2026111111065697
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-110656
	- Opened by: t00mietum
	- Prereq IDs: 2026111111065684
	- Requirements:
		- Default configuration hard-coded.
		- Overridden by the config file.
		- Resistant to errors, so one bad line doesn't sink the whole thing.
		- Overridden by program options at run-time.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Decisions:
		- Settings are built in order: the defaults, the file, the command-line options, then the keys that come from others, such as `runGroup` from `runUser`. Deriving last means an option that changes `runUser` moves `runGroup` too.
		- Options are checked by the same code as the file. A bad one is an error, not a warning, since it was typed just now.
		- design.md now has a common `--set KEY=VALUE`, with `--native` as a short form for `runMode`. The flags themselves are parsed in the Core commands item, which builds the CLI.
		- `heapMin` defaults to 1G, or `heapMax` when that's less. A `heapMin` over `heapMax`, or a `memCap` not over it, gets a warning and a safe value.
		- `ownerAfterStop` comes from `SUDO_USER`, then `USER`. It's left empty when neither names an account, as from the boot unit, and Core commands decides what `stop` does then.
		- The default image is `eclipse-temurin:25-jre`, pinned to its multi-arch digest. `jvmArgs` defaults to Paper's recommended flags.
		- An option value follows file syntax, so `##` starts a comment there too.
		- Swept: every default in design.md's table that's written as a value is checked against the code by a test. The derived ones have their own cases.
		- 20261111: The Check picks stand: `--set KEY=VALUE`, the pinned `eclipse-temurin:25-jre` image, and Paper's recommended `jvmArgs`.
	- Branch: r1111
	- Test case: 1a3ef6r, 1a3ef9w, 1a3efd0, 1a3efg4, 1a3efk8, 1a3efpc
	- Closed: 20261111-121403

- Core commands: CLI, command runner, and tree hand-off
	- ID: 2026111112162268
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-121622
	- Opened by: t00mietum
	- Parent ID: 2026111111065711
	- Requirements:
		- Parse the commands and the common options, `--set` and `--native` included, and load the config.
		- Add `--help`, `--about`, `--version`, `--dry-run`, and `--debug`.
		- One command runner, with sudo and dry run, and a fake for tests.
		- Hand the trees to the service account and back.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Decisions:
		- Common options work before or after the command. `--native` and `--set` reach the config in the order given, so the last one wins.
		- A missing default config means the defaults, with a warning, so `status` works before `setup`. A missing `--config` file is an error, exit 1. A bad option is exit 2.
		- `--dry-run` still runs commands that only look, such as `docker ps`, so the plan it prints matches a real run. design.md updated.
		- With `ownerAfterStop` empty, as from the boot unit, `stop` leaves the trees with `runUser` and warns. The operator's next `stop` hands them back, per design.md's `stop` step 1.
		- A tree given as a link is followed before `chown -R`, and the depth check runs on both the link and its target.
		- `--help` lists only the built commands. `start`, `stop`, `status`, and `watch` parse and load the config, then say they aren't built yet, until the next 3 children.
		- Swept: both flag passes share `commonFlags`; every chown goes through `tree.handOver`.
		- 20261111: Kept after the Check: `--dry-run` still runs the commands that only look, the rules for a missing config, and `stop` with `ownerAfterStop` empty.
	- Branch: r1111
	- Test case: 1a3efsg, 1a3efwm, 1a3efzr, 1a3eg2w, 1a3eg60, 1a3eg94, 1a3egc8, 1a3egfc, 1a3egjg, 1a3egnm
	- Closed: 20261111-122043

- Safe server settings
	- ID: 2026111111065724
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-110657
	- Opened by: t00mietum
	- Prereq IDs: 2026111112163174
	- Requirements:
		- `start` checks the settings listed in `design.md` and refuses when one is wrong. `repair` fixes them.
		- Force online mode and the whitelist on. Force RCON, query, the management server, and the proxy settings off.
		- Warn about an empty whitelist, and about more than 2 ops.
	- Prereq moved from the Core commands parent to its Docker mode child, which is all this needs to start.
	- Split: the Geyser rows are in child 2026111112554261, which is Done. The rest is done too.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Progress log:
		- 2026-11-11: `start` checks `server.properties`, `spigot.yml`, and `paper-global.yml` in both modes, and refuses with the setting named. `repair` sets them, and `repair --check` only reports. The whitelist and ops warnings show at `start` and `repair`.
		- Verified: each new test fails with its check broken, and passes with it in place.
	- Decisions:
		- `proxies.proxy-protocol` is forced off too, as a proxy setting. design.md updated, with a note under Needs more thought for tunnels.
		- A missing `server.properties` is unsafe, since Minecraft's defaults have the whitelist off. `repair` writes one. A missing YAML file or key is fine, since each defaults to off.
		- Booleans compare without case, as Java reads them. A fix changes only the wrong lines, and keeps comments, order, owner, and mode.
		- Files are opened without following links, and a fix writes a new file and renames it into place, since the server account can write the tree and `repair` runs as root.
		- `repair` is only the safe settings check so far. The repair item adds the rest. It refuses without root unless `--check` is given.
		- `status` now reads the whitelist and ops through the same code.
		- The empty whitelist warning names `tt-minecraftd cmd whitelist add`, per design.md, though `cmd` comes with its own item.
		- Swept: both modes call the check through `Kit`; `status` and the warnings share one player reader.
		- 20261111: Kept after the Check: `proxy-protocol` off, and a missing `server.properties` counted as unsafe. `repair` no longer refuses without root, and runs itself through sudo instead, see 2026111111065763.
	- Branch: r1111
	- Test case: its children's: 2026111112554261: 1a3emcc, 1a3emfg, 1a3emjm
	- Closed: 20261111-130256

- Safe server settings: Geyser config
	- ID: 2026111112554261
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-125542
	- Opened by: t00mietum
	- Parent ID: 2026111111065724
	- Requirements:
		- With `bedrock` on, `start` checks Geyser's `config.yml` and `repair` sets it: `bedrock.address` and `bedrock.port` from `bindIP` and `bedrockPort`, and `auth-type` set to `floodgate`, per `design.md`.
		- Check the key names against the Geyser build in use, since its config layout has changed between versions. `auth-type` has sat at the top level, under `remote:`, and under `java:`.
		- Use the YAML and file code in `source/internal/settings`.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Progress log:
		- 2026-11-11: `start` checks `plugins/Geyser-Spigot/config.yml` with `bedrock` on, and `repair` sets `bedrock.address`, `bedrock.port`, and `auth-type`, adding a key that's missing. Key names checked against Geyser 2.12.1 (build 1299), whose config migrations run to `config-version` 12.
		- Verified: each new test fails with its check broken, and passes with it in place.
	- Decisions:
		- A missing `config.yml` is fine. Geyser writes it at its first start with online-mode logins on all addresses, which isn't unsafe, and the next start checks it. design.md updated.
		- `auth-type` is found by the file's layout: `java:` from `config-version` 5, then `remote:`, then the top level. Geyser's own migration renames `remote` to `java` at version 5.
		- A missing key is wrong, since Geyser's defaults don't follow the config. `repair` adds it at the end of its section.
		- All addresses is written as `0.0.0.0`, Geyser's own default. An IPv6 address is quoted. `auth-type` compares without case.
		- Swept: `Check` and `Fix` share one scan, so `start` through `Kit` and `repair` both get it. The Floodgate config and `key.pem` mode are left to the repair item, per design.md.
	- Branch: r1111
	- Test case: 1a3emcc, 1a3emfg, 1a3emjm
	- Closed: 20261111-130203

- Plugin pinning
	- ID: 2026111111065737
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-110657
	- Opened by: t00mietum
	- Prereq IDs: 2026111112163174
	- Requirements:
		- Keep the approved jars and their hashes in `/etc/tt-minecraftd/plugins.shcl`.
		- `start` refuses when a jar is new or changed.
		- `repair --approve-plugins` shows each one and asks.
		- The server can't write to the jars while it runs.
	- Prereq moved from the Core commands parent to its Docker mode child, which is all this needs to start.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Progress log:
		- Verified: `start` refuses a new or changed plugin, extension, or Paper build in both modes, and the start test fails with the check taken out. `repair --approve-plugins` approves, refuses, and leaves unanswered jars as asked.
	- Decisions:
		- One list of checked jars, `plugins.Jars`, feeds `start`, `status`, `repair`, and the read-only mounts, so they can't disagree. Geyser extensions are now locked like plugins. design.md updated.
		- `paper.jar` is checked as the build it links to, by that build's path under `serverDir`. One that links outside can't be approved, so `start` refuses.
		- No approved list yet means every jar is new.
		- Answers are `y` or `n`, asked again on anything else. When input runs out, the jars left stay put and unapproved, and `repair` exits 1. A refused Paper build isn't moved, since that only breaks `paper.jar`.
		- The list is updated in place through shcl, so comments and sources stay. A new list is mode 0644, since `status` reads it without sudo.
		- `plugins-refused/` takes the owner of `plugins/`. A name already there gets the time added.
		- The name and version come from `paper-plugin.yml`, `plugin.yml`, or `extension.yml`, read up to 64 KiB, with control characters dropped.
		- `--approve-plugins` with `--check` is a usage error. No `--yes` for it, since the point is a person looking at each jar.
		- 20261111: the status tests' approved list gained the Paper build, and their jar counts went from 2 to 3 and from 3 to 4 (1a3ejdw, 1a3ejm4, 1a3ekd4), since `paper.jar` is now counted.
		- Swept: every reader of jars goes through `plugins.Jars` (`CheckJars`, `lockedJars`, `repair`); both modes call `Kit.checkPlugins`; status reads the list through the same path as `start`.
	- Branch: r1111
	- Test case: 1a3emw0, 1a3emz4, 1a3en28, 1a3en5c, 1a3en8g, 1a3enbm. Plugin approval is gone since 2026111914334569: 1a3en28, 1a3en5c, 1a3en8g and 1a3enbm are commented out.
	- Closed: 20261111-131704

- repair
	- ID: 2026111111065763
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Closed
	- Closed reason: Done
	- Priority: High
	- Opened: 20261111-110657
	- Opened by: t00mietum
	- Prereq IDs: 2026111111065724, 2026111111065737
	- Requirements:
		- Run the checks listed in `design.md`, each with an optional fix.
		- `--check` only reports.
	- Actual effort: Avg
	- Progress log:
		- 2026-11-11: `repair` runs design.md's checks 1 to 8, 10, 11, 12, and 14 in order, around the safe settings and plugin steps. Each one prints OK, FIXED, or FAILED, and `--check` fixes nothing.
		- Verified: each new test fails with its check broken, and passes with it in place.
		- 20261111: Verified: 1a3em64 fails with the old refusal put back, and with `--dry-run` taken out, and passes with both in.
	- Decisions:
		- Checks 9 and 13 come with the Firewall and Start at boot items, which list `repair` managing them. There's no rules file or boot unit to check yet.
		- Checks are a list in `source/internal/repair`, each with a look and an optional fix per finding, per design.md. Every fix is a command through the runner.
		- A `runUser` that's root or uid 1000 and up is reported and left alone, since stripping its groups and login would lock a person out. design.md updated.
		- A new account is `useradd --system`, home `/nonexistent`, shell `/usr/sbin/nologin`. "Can't log in" is checked by its shell. The password field needs `/etc/shadow`, which `--check` can't read.
		- Tree modes are checked only while the server runs, since `stop` only hands back ownership. Native mode's root-owned jars are left out while it runs. design.md updated.
		- The config check reports a missing config, since `setup` writes it. The server folder, `paper.jar`, Geyser, and Floodgate are reported, not fixed, since `setup` and `update` fetch them.
		- The Floodgate key is fixed with `chmod o-rwx`. Its group is the tree ownership check's. design.md names no Floodgate config keys, so none are pinned.
		- In Docker mode a missing image is pulled, and an old stopped container is removed, per design.md.
		- ufw and firewalld rules are read only as root. Under `--check` without root, an active one is reported as unread. firewalld's default zone ports are read, so a port opened through a service shows as missing. design.md updated.
		- Other Paper servers are told apart by uid, since both modes run the server under `runUser`'s uid on the host.
		- `launch.LockedJars` is exported, and `launch.Running` and `server.PaperProcs` added, so `repair` shares them with `start` and `status`.
		- Swept: `refuseOthers` and `status` still go through `RunningPaper`, which now uses `PaperProcs`; both modes' jar locks use `LockedJars`.
		- 20261111: Without root and without `--check`, `repair` runs itself again through sudo, as `setup` and `update` do. It used to refuse. `--dry-run` is the same as `--check`. design.md listed a `--yes` for it, which is gone, since its only questions are per jar.
	- Branch: r1111, repair-sudo
	- Test case: 1a3env8, 1a3enyc, 1a3ep1g, 1a3ep4m, 1a3ep7r, 1a3epaw, 1a3epe0, 1a3eph4, 1a3epm8, 1a3epqc, 1a3eptg, 1a3epxm
	- Closed: 20261111-133545

- Code review 20261121: observations not filed
	- ID: 2026112110300442
	- Type: Task
	- Status: Canceled
	- Opened: 20261121-103000
	- Opened by:
	- Decided against: `gfs-rotate.bash` against `oldbuild.Rotate`. Both keep the same files, and the script is used only on CICD's own folders.
	- Decided against: The gap between `download.Staged.Keep`'s check and its rename. update refuses while the server runs, so nothing of the server account's is running then.
	- Decided against: A config path with a newline breaking the unit's ExecStart. The operator picks the path.
	- Decided against: Old build names stamped under a different TZ, or in a DST overlap. At most an hour off in the rotation.
	- Decided against: `Rotate` pruning hand-placed `tt-minecraftd.*` files. The folder is the tool's, and a test pins it.
	- Decided against: selfupdate not retiring the old build. It's deferred, and design.md says it gets the move when it comes back.
	- Decided against: install.bash's release list parse leaning on GitHub's pretty-printed JSON. It matches the API today.
	- Decided against: ufw's v6-only allow counted for IPv4. A test pins it as meant.
	- Decided against: `plugins.Libraries` keyed to the old `paper.jar`. Paperclip writes its own libraries back from its jar.
	- Decided against: `rm -f` in the library removal. It runs as runUser on full paths after `--`.
	- Decided against: The native ownership fix handing locked jars back to runUser. Native mode is off, and the unit keeps them read-only.
	- Decided against: `linksafe.OpenFile` opening without O_PATH. The container drops all capabilities, and a FIFO opened with O_NONBLOCK is harmless.
	- Decided against: A backslash in a jar name making `SetSources` refuse the batch. Too contrived.
	- Decided against: A folder swap after `Mkdir`'s mkdirat. The server can only swap in a folder it could already reach.
	- Decided against: Hard links in the tree. `protected_hardlinks` limits them to files the server can already reach.
	- Decided against: Geyser `auth-type` with no `config-version`, both top-level and under `java:`. Crafted only, and offline logins on an online-mode Java server let nobody in.
	- Decided against: `config-version` written as hex or `5.0`, `!!str false`, and mixed-case `FaLsE`. Contrived, or Paper falls back to its default of false.
	- Decided against: `agent.Check` cutting `sha256` and `url` to length before it checks them. The download hash check still refuses a wrong one.
	- Decided against: The GitHub content hosts in `agentHosts`. Same class as the hostile agent on github.com, decided on 2026111715280984.
	- Decided against: Each jar hashed 2 or 3 times a run. Plugin jars are small.
	- Decided against: `extraArgs` able to override the safe settings. It's the root-owned config's choice, per design.md.
	- Decided against: `agent/live_test.go` not driving the `command` kind. Test only.
	- Decided against: The 401 hint naming only `TT_MINECRAFTD_AGENT_KEY`. Cosmetic.
	- Decided against: Firewall asymmetry between IPv4 and IPv6. A namespace probe of 18 targets under `egress: web` with Bedrock on gave the same answer for both.
	- Decided against: Spoofing into `seek4`. It takes a LAN accomplice, and only UDP from 7551 gets through.
	- Decided against: Global IPv6 LAN clients refused under `lan`, public on-link IPv4 subnets not blocked, and NAT64 not in `lan6`. design.md sets those ranges.
	- Decided against: start refusing after a new ISP IPv6 prefix until repair. That's the design's rule.
	- Decided against: `LogThread`'s greedy match on chat text, and a late answer to an async command being cut off. False negatives only, and design.md counts only server-thread lines.
	- Decided against: Podman's `stopping` or `paused` state counting as not running. Only happens by hand.
	- Decided against: The CodeQL CSV loop and a last line with no newline. CodeQL ends every row with one.
	- Decided against: `CICD_FUZZ_TIME` or `CICD_SOAK_ROUNDS` weakening a merge gate that still writes its marker. Deliberate operator overrides.
	- Decided against: `fGoTestRun` not failing on 0 passed lines. Areas are checked against real folders, and test-id.bash finds missing IDs.
	- Decided against: Small uncapped `docker run --rm` helper calls on the cache volume. Tiny and short-lived.
	- Decided against: `fSweepRuns` removing a kept `--zfs` run folder while the pool is imported. ZFS holds the file open.
	- Decided against: verify-release.bash not clearing `go env -w` settings. An odd setup.
	- Decided against: Lint leads `agent.go` badCond, `launch/links.go` nilerr, `settings/yaml.go` nilnil, and the hugeParam notes. Each is meant, or costs nothing.
	- Decided against: The 2026-11-18 line length directive. MD013 is off, and no other width is set in the repo.

- Code review 20261117: observations not filed
	- ID: 2026111715280984
	- Type: Task
	- Status: Canceled
	- Opened: 20261117-152809
	- Opened by:
	- Decided against: Unchecked Close in plugins/approve.go, plugins/check.go, repair/files.go and update/agent.go. Each file is only read.
	- Decided against: The unused uid and gid starting values in linksafe.go. Both branches set them.
	- Decided against: No unit test for paper.fchownatNoFollow. It doesn't follow the temp link, and the root host check covers paper.jar.
	- Decided against: linksafe.Write keeping setuid and setgid bits from the old file. The new file has the old file's owner, so nothing is gained.
	- Decided against: chmod -R in the tree hand-off following a link. It's given the resolved real tree, and its parents aren't writable by the server.
	- Decided against: The RCON drop skipped when a game port is 25575. That's needed, and a test pins it.
	- Decided against: Rate limiting per IPv6 address rather than per /64. It's a recorded decision on the Firewall item.
	- Decided against: The append in launch/session.go that lint flags. No backing array is shared.
	- Decided against: The missing switch cases in launch/group.go. Unknown and here are meant to pass.
	- Decided against: Operator args and environment reaching the sudo run. sudo resets the environment, and the operator already has sudo.
	- Decided against: config switches without array and raw cases. The default branch warns.
	- Decided against: The dropped MarshalIndent error in agent.go. The question holds only strings, so it can't fail.
	- Decided against: The agent answer file handed to the sudo run. Its folder is 0700 and only the operator can write it, and root checks everything again.
	- Decided against: A hostile agent hosting a hash on github.com. The plugin name match and the operator's yes are the gate, as design.md says.
	- Decided against: update exiting 0 when every agent jar fails a check. design.md says so.
	- Decided against: Unsigned releases. design.md lists signing under Needs more thought.
	- Decided against: install-dev.bash counting Podman without podman-docker as a container runtime. 2026111713320657 asked for that.
	- Decided against: A runUser that names an existing service account losing its groups. The operator chose it, and people's accounts are guarded.
	- Decided against: Unchecked Fprintf to the terminal, about 130 sites. Nothing can be done on a failed terminal write.
	- Decided against: shellcheck with every optional check on. It found only SC2310 and SC2312 notes.

- setup and repair offer zfs allow for snapshots
	- ID: 2026111211050654
	- Type: Feature
	- Status: Canceled
	- Priority: Low
	- Opened: 20261112-110506
	- Opened by:
	- Prereq IDs: 2026111112264283
	- Requirements:
		- With `zfsDataset` set, `setup` offers `zfs allow` for snapshot, destroy, and mount on the dataset to the operator, per design.md's ZFS snapshots, so snapshots don't need sudo.
		- `repair` reports it when it's missing.
	- Decisions:
		- 20261113: Canceled. Snapshots are taken only before `--mc-version` now, which is rare and runs through sudo anyway.

## Template

- Notes:

	- Only use rows that you actually need or expect will be filled in. Always fill in the title, ID, Type, Status, Opened and Created by.

	- The ID is the local time to the hundredth of a second. Opened is when it was written down, which may differ. (Use a keyboard macro and possibly something like project 'zuid' to generate.)

	- Status values meaning: Testing means the fix is in and checks are running or still to run. Waiting on signoff means automated testing passed. Canceled means it still applies but was decided against, and it needs no reason. Waiting for testing means the fix is in and waits on a long CI run or an outside test host. Closed means no more work is planned, and Closed reason says why.

	- Closed reason values: Done means the work is finished. Duplicate means another item covers it, and Superseded means a newer item replaces it. Both name that item in Superseded by ID. Could not reproduce means a real attempt to reproduce it failed. Works as intended means the report describes how it's meant to work. Won't fix means it's a real bug that's left as is, and the item has to say why. Moot means something else changed that made it irrelevant.

	- Target version is the release an item is meant for. Every open item has one, and so does every item closed since the last release.

	- As issues are worked, and statuses change, place them in correct sorting order within the list:
		- First by status: Waiting for answers, Waiting on signoff, Testing, Waiting for testing, Stalled, Started, Queued, Closed, Deferred, Canceled
		- Then by severity|priority: Critical, High, Avg, Low
		- Then by type: Bugs, [not bugs together]

	- Rows marked [Bug] are for bugs only, and rows marked [Feature] for features and enhancements. Priority and Severity share one row and one scale. Priority is for a Feature or Enhancement, and Severity for a Bug. Children are not nested. They sit at the top level and point back with Parent ID.

Template:

- Title
	- ID: YYYYmmDDHHMMSSNN
	- Type: [Bug|Feature|Enhancement|Task]
	- Status: [Queued|Waiting for answers|Waiting on signoff|Waiting for testing|Started|Testing|Stalled|Closed|Canceled|Deferred]
	- Closed reason: [Done|Duplicate|Superseded|Could not reproduce|Works as intended|Won't fix|Moot]
	- Target version:
	- Needs local test suite run?:
	- Needs external testing:
	- Priority [Feature|Enhancement] | Severity [Bug]: [Critical|High|Avg|Low]
	- Opened:
	- Opened by:
	- Assigned to:
	- Parent ID:
	- Prereq IDs:
	- Related IDs:
	- Target OS:
	- Test environment:
	- Version and build:
	- Requirements  [Feature]:
		- Hierarchical bulleted list.
	- Steps to reproduce [Bug]:
		- ...
	- Incorrect behavior [Bug]:
	- Expected behavior [Bug]:
	- Reproduced [Bug]: [No, or when, where and how]
	- Possible cause [Bug]:
	- Actual cause [Bug]:
		- ...
	- Estimated effort: [High|Avg|Low]
	- Actual effort: [High|Avg|Low]
	- Progress log:
		- ...
	- Decisions:
		- ...
	- Actual fix [Bug]:
	- Branch:
	- Commit:
	- Test case: [Reason not applicable, or CI test case #]
	- Acceptance signoff:
	- Superseded by ID:
	- Closed:
